A failure counts as an invalid call (FR-12).\"\nrep(anchor, anchor+'''\n\n*As built (WP-21):*\n- **Spike (crt.sh and status pages, 2026-10-16):** an exact-host crt.sh query doesn't find wildcard certificates (the demo's `*.<sub>.workers.dev` came back empty), so `cert_info` also asks for `*.parent` (never `*.tld`), and runs both in parallel. crt.sh took 2–12 s, sometimes 404s, and timed out at 30 s for cloudflare.com. `expired.badssl.com` is covered by a **valid** `*.badssl.com` wildcard in the CT logs: CT shows issued certificates, not the one being served, so it can't catch that kind of expiry. Statuspage answers are a few KB in under a second. AWS's `health.aws.amazon.com/public/currentevents` is 231 KB of UTF-16 (big-endian, with a BOM) JSON; it's read up to 128 KB and the events are picked out of the part that fits.\n- **`cert_info` (`tools/cert.ts`):** `exclude=expired&deduplicate=Y`, 256 KB per answer, at most 20 entries, a cut-off body parsed up to its last complete entry. Matching certificates first (exact SAN or a one-label wildcard), most days left first, at most 3. `pass` when the best one has more than 7 days left; `fail` when it expires within 7 days or no unexpired certificate covers the host; `unavailable` when crt.sh doesn't answer (all queries), or when one query failed and nothing matched. Answers are cached for an hour (`caches.default`, `ToolContext.cache` in tests); any cache error is ignored.\n- **`provider_status` (`tools/provider.ts`):** fixed URLs (`www.cloudflarestatus.com`, `www.githubstatus.com`, AWS's feed), 5 s each, in parallel. Each incident keeps `impact` when Statuspage gives one. **Only major or critical incidents count as an outage** (`isSignificant`): during the spike Cloudflare had a minor \"Dashboard sign up issues\" incident open, and the demo itself answers with `server: cloudflare`, so every broken demo would have looked like a Cloudflare outage. AWS events have no impact and always count. A provider that doesn't answer is listed in `unavailable`; the check is `unavailable` only if none answered.\n- **Reader:** `readOnce` takes a `decode` option (UTF-8 by default) for AWS's UTF-16.''')\nanchor=\"- **Card before narration (WP-13b).**\"\nrep(anchor,'''- **Automatic cert/provider checks (WP-21, user decision \"only when useful\").** After the baseline (step `pending-extras`, turn 98) and in the rule path (step `pending-rules-extras`, turn 100), `loop/extras.ts` `extraCalls` adds `cert_info` for the host when the target is HTTPS and an `http_probe` sample failed with a network error (how an expired or self-signed certificate looks on workerd), and `provider_status` for the fingerprinted providers when HTTP is failing, **except on the user's own demo** (its 500s are ours). The signals come from the tool step's own result (`httpSignals`), so a replay sees the same; CNAME fingerprints use the run's CNAME targets. They count against the tool cap, and the model can still call either tool itself. `fingerprint()` moved to `diagnosis/fingerprint.ts`, shared with rule 5.\n'''+anchor)\nrep(\"| 2 | `tls_expiry` | Fetch fails with a TLS error, or the best certificate has `daysToExpiry` < 0 (≤ 7 days gives `medium` and only applies if HTTP is otherwise healthy) | high / medium |\",\n\"| 2 | `tls_expiry` | Fetch fails with a TLS error, or the best certificate has `daysToExpiry` < 0 (≤ 7 days gives `medium` and only applies if HTTP is otherwise healthy). *WP-21:* an expired best certificate counts only when HTTP isn't fully healthy, and an HTTPS target refusing every connection while no unexpired certificate covers it in the CT logs gives `medium` | high / medium |\")\nrep(\"| 5 | `provider_outage` | HTTP is failing **and** the provider whose fingerprint matches (`server`/`cf-ray` for Cloudflare, `*.github.io` CNAME for GitHub, `amazonaws` CNAME for AWS) has an active incident | medium |\",\n\"| 5 | `provider_outage` | HTTP is failing **and** the provider whose fingerprint matches (`server`/`cf-ray` for Cloudflare, `*.github.io` CNAME for GitHub, `amazonaws` CNAME for AWS) has an active incident (*WP-21:* major or critical; import type { StreamFrame } from "../../worker/agent/state";

/** Live narration text per message, built from the agent's WebSocket side channel (TDD §6.3). */
export type StreamBuffer = { messageId: string; text: string; done: boolean };
export type Streams = Readonly<Record<string, StreamBuffer>>;

/** Our stream frames only; the SDK's own `cf_agent_*` and `rpc` frames return null. */
export function parseFrame(data: unknown): StreamFrame | null {
  if (typeof data !== "string") return null;
  let f: unknown;
  try {
    f = JSON.parse(data);
  } catch {
    return null;
  }
  if (typeof f !== "object" || f === null) return null;
  const { type, messageId, text } = f as Record<string, unknown>;
  if (typeof messageId !== "string") return null;
  if (type === "stream_start" || type === "stream_end") return { type, messageId };
  if (type === "delta" && typeof text === "string") return { type, messageId, text };
  return null;
}

/** `stream_start` resets (a retried narration streams again from the start). */
export function applyFrame(streams: Streams, f: StreamFrame): Streams {
  const current = streams[f.messageId];
  switch (f.type) {
    case "stream_start":
      return { ...streams, [f.messageId]: { messageId: f.messageId, text: "", done: false } };
    case "delta":
      return { ...streams, [f.messageId]: { messageId: f.messageId, text: (current?.text ?? "") + f.text, done: false } };
    case "stream_end":
      return current ? { ...streams, [f.messageId]: { ...current, done: true } } : streams;
  }
}
```

import type { IncidentStatus, RunStatus, TimelineView } from "../../worker/agent/state";
import { parseTarget } from "../../worker/safety/target";

/**
 * A follow-up that names a different host than the incident's (TDD §8.6). Tools are pinned to the incident's
 * origin (D6), so the UI offers a new incident instead of sending. "demo" alone doesn't count.
 */
export function otherHost(text: string, incidentHost: string): string | null {
  const r = parseTarget(text, "https://demo.invalid/");
  if (!r.ok || r.source === "demo") return null;
  return r.host === incidentHost.toLowerCase() ? null : r.host;
}

\"Follow-up question asked\" : null;\n    case \"findings\": {\n      const category = CATEGORY_LABEL[str(d.category) as keyof typeof CATEGORY_LABEL] ?? str(d.category);\n      return `Diagnosis: ${category} (${str(d.confidence)} confidence${d.mode === \"rule_based\" ? \", rule-based\" : \"\"})`;\n    }\n    case \"approval\":\n      switch (d.status) {\n        case \"pending\":\n          return `Proposed: ${title()}`;\n        case \"approved\":\n          return `Approved: ${title()}`;\n        case \"rejected\":\n          return `Rejected: ${title()}`;\n        case \"expired\":\n          return `Expired without a decision: ${title()}`;\n        case \"superseded\":\n          return `Replaced by a newer proposal: ${title()}`;\n        default:\n          return null;\n      }\n    case \"action_executed\":\n      return d.ok ? `Action ran: ${title()}` : `Action failed: ${title()}`;\n    case \"verified\":\n      return d.pass ? `Verified after the action: HTTP ${str(d.status)} in ${str(d.ms)} ms` : \"Re-check after the action still failing\";\n    case \"status\":\n      return `Status ${str(d.from).replace(/_/g, \" \")} → ${str(d.to).replace(/_/g, \" \")}${d.reason ? ` (${str(d.reason)})` : \"\"}`;\n    default:\n      return null;\n  }\n}\n\n/** The prose without an AI: from the findings, the approvals and how the incident ended. */\nexport function templateProse(p: PostmortemInput, facts: Facts): Prose {\n  const f = p.findings;\n  const category = f ? CATEGORY_LABEL[f.category] : \"an unknown problem\";\n  const opened = p.origin === \"watch\" ? \"a failed watch check\" : \"a chat report\";\n  const took = facts.durationMs !== null ? ` and was resolved after ${duration(facts.durationMs)}` : \"\";\n  const actions = p.approvals.filter((a) => a.status === \"approved\");\n  const resolution =\n    facts.resolvedBy === \"hand\"\n      ? \"The incident was resolved by hand.\"\n      : actions.length\n        ? actions.map((a) => `${a.title} (approved). ${a.outcome ?? \"\"}`.trim()).join(\"\\n\\n\")\n        : facts.resolvedBy === \"checks\"\n          ? \"The endpoint recovered: 3 checks in a row passed.\"\n          : \"No action was taken.\";\n  return {\n    summary: `${facts.host} had ${f ? `${category.toLowerCase() === category ? category : category} (${f.confidence} confidence)` : category}. The incident was opened from ${opened}${took}.`,\n    rootCause: f?.cause ?? \"No diagnosis was recorded.\",\n    resolution,\n    followUps: (f?.next_steps.length ? f.next_steps : [\"Review the evidence and decide whether a follow-up is needed.\"]).map((s) => `- ${s}`).join(\"\\n\"),\n  };\n}\n\nconst HEADING = /^#{1,3}\\s*(.+?)\\s*:?\\s*$/;\nconst KEY_OF: [RegExp, keyof Prose][] = [\n  [/^summary$/i, \"summary\"],\n  [/^root\\s*cause/i, \"rootCause\"],\n  [/^resolution/i, \"resolution\"],\n  [/^follow[\\s-]*ups?|^action items/i, \"followUps\"],\n];\n\n/**\n * The AI's four `##` sections. Full-width `【id】` citations are normalised and unknown evidence IDs removed; a\n * section that's missing or empty comes from `fallback`, so the result always has all four.\n */\nexport function parseProse(text: string, knownIds: readonly string[], fallback: Prose): { prose: Prose; fromAi: number } {\n  const known = new Set(knownIds);\n  const clean = normalizeRefs(text).replace(/\\[([a-z]+-\\d+)\\]/g, (m, id: string) => (known.has(id) ? m : \"\"));\n  const found: Partial<Prose> = {};\n  let key: keyof Prose | null = null;\n  let body: string[] = [];\n  const close = () => {\n    if (key && body.join(\"\\n\").trim()) found[key] ??= body.join(\"\\n\").trim();\n    body = [];\n  };\n  for (const line of clean.split(\"\\n\")) {\n    const h = HEADING.exec(line.trim());\n    const k = h ? KEY_OF.find(([re]) => re.test(h[1]!.replace(/\\*\\*/g, \"\").trim()))?.[1] : undefined;\n    if (h) {\n      close();\n      key = k ?? null;\n      continue;\n    }\n    if (key) body.push(line);\n  }\n  close();\n  const prose = Object.fromEntries(PROSE_SECTIONS.map((k) => [k, found[k] ?? fallback[k]])) as Prose;\n  return { prose, fromAi: PROSE_SECTIONS.filter((k) => found[k]).length };\n}\n\nexport function renderPostmortem(facts: Facts, prose: Prose): string {\n  return [\n    `# Postmortem: ${facts.host}`,\n    `## ${HEADINGS.summary}\\n\\n${prose.summary}`,\n    `## Impact window\\n\\n${facts.impact.join(\"\\n\")}`,\n    `## Detection\\n\\n${facts.detection}`,\n    `## Timeline (UTC)\\n\\n${facts.timeline.length ? facts.timeline.join(\"\\n\") : \"- No events recorded.\"}`,\n    `## ${HEADINGS.rootCause}\\n\\n${prose.rootCause}`,\n    `## ${HEADINGS.resolution}\\n\\n${prose.resolution}`,\n    `## ${HEADINGS.followUps}\\n\\n${prose.followUps}`,\n  ].join(\"\\n\\n\");\n}\n\n/** The facts as plain text for the AI prompt. */\nexport function factsText(facts: Facts): string {\n  return [`Impact window:\\n${facts.impact.join(\"\\n\")}`, `Detection: ${facts.detection}`, `Timeline (UTC):\\n${facts.timeline.join(\"\\n\")}`].join(\"\\n\\n\");\n}\nEOF\nnpx tsc -p tsconfig.worker.json --noEmit 2>&1 | head",
  "description": "Write the postmortem builder"
}

  target: `${DEMO}/w/${WS}/`,
  workspaceId: WS,
  demoBaseUrl: DEMO,
  category: "origin_5xx",
  status: "investigating",

import { exports } from "cloudflare:workers";

export type Frame = { type: string; [k: string]: unknown };

/** A raw agent WebSocket that speaks the SDK's frames: `cf_agent_state`, `rpc`, plus our stream frames. */
export async function connect(incidentId: string, workspaceId?: string, headers: Record<string, string> = {}) {
  const res = await exports.default.fetch(`https://copilot.example.com/agents/incident-agent/${incidentId}`, {
    headers: { Upgrade: "websocket", ...(workspaceId ? { Cookie: `__Host-ws=${workspaceId}` } : {}), ...headers },
  });
  if (res.status !== 101 || !res.webSocket) throw new Error(`upgrade failed: ${res.status}`);
  const ws = res.webSocket;
  ws.accept();
  const frames: Frame[] = [];
  const waiters: { match: (f: Frame) => boolean; resolve: (f: Frame) => void }[] = [];
  let closed: { code: number; reason: string } | null = null;
  ws.addEventListener("message", (e) => {
    const frame = JSON.parse(String(e.data)) as Frame;
    frames.push(frame);
    for (const w of [...waiters]) {
      if (w.match(frame)) {
        waiters.splice(waiters.indexOf(w), 1);
        w.resolve(frame);
      }
    }
  });
  ws.addEventListener("close", (e) => {
    closed = { code: e.code, reason: e.reason };
  });

  const next = (match: (f: Frame) => boolean, ms = 3000) =>
    new Promise<Frame>((resolve, reject) => {
      const seen = frames.find(match);
      if (seen) return resolve(seen);
      const timer = setTimeout(() => reject(new Error("timed out waiting for a frame")), ms);
      waiters.push({ match, resolve: (f) => (clearTimeout(timer), resolve(f)) });
    });

  let n = 0;
  return {
    frames,
    closed: () => closed,
    /** Resolves with `{success, result | error}`. */
    rpc: async (method: string, args: unknown[] = []) => {
      const id = `t${++n}`;
      ws.send(JSON.stringify({ type: "rpc", id, method, args }));
      return (await next((f) => f.type === "rpc" && f.id === id)) as Frame & { success: boolean; result?: unknown; error?: string };
    },
    send: (frame: unknown) => ws.send(JSON.stringify(frame)),
    next,
    /** The latest synced state (the SDK sends it on connect and after every change). */
    state: async () => (await next((f) => f.type === "cf_agent_state")).state as Record<string, unknown>,
    close: () => ws.close(1000, "done"),
  };
}
import { env, exports } from "cloudflare:workers";

const TARGET = "https://shop.example.com/";

/** The message of a rejected RPC call (awaiting the RPC promise directly). */
async function errorOf(p: PromiseLike<unknown>): Promise<string> {
  try {
    await p;
  } catch (e) {
    return (e as Error).message;
  }
  throw new Error("expected the call to fail");
}

async function newIncident(message = "why is shop.example.com down?") {
  const id = newId();
  const ws = newId();
  const agent = await incidentAgent(env, id);
  const { runId } = await agent.init({ workspaceId: ws, target: TARGET, origin: "chat", message });
  return { id, ws, agent, runId: runId! };
}

};

/** Plays one Workflow run against the agent: plan → checks → findings. */
async function playRun(agent: Awaited<ReturnType<typeof incidentAgent>>, runId: string, f: Findings = findings, mode: "ai" | "rule_based" = "ai") {
  const ids = await agent.addPendingChecks(runId, 1, [
    { tool: "dns_lookup", args: { hostname: "shop.example.com" } },
    { tool: "http_probe", args: { url: TARGET } },
  ]);
  await agent.recordCheck(ids[0]!, { ...dnsOk, evidenceId: ids[0]! });
  await agent.recordCheck(ids[1]!, { ...http500, evidenceId: ids[1]! });
  await agent.setFindings(runId, f, mode);
  return ids;
}

import { env, exports } from "cloudflare:workers";
import { afterEach, describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import { incidentAgent } from "../../worker/agent/stub";
import { newId } from "../../worker/lib/ids";
import { BUDGET_GUARD_AT } from "../../worker/loop/budget";

// D1 is per test file, so the forced total here can't reach other files' runs; it's cleared after each test.
const BASE = "https://copilot.example.com";
const today = () => new Date().toISOString().slice(0, 10);
const setToday = (neurons: number) =>
  env.DB.prepare("INSERT INTO llm_usage (day, neurons_est) VALUES (?1, ?2) ON CONFLICT(day) DO UPDATE SET neurons_est = excluded.neurons_est")
    .bind(today(), neurons)
    .run();

async function budgetMode(): Promise<string> {
  return (await (await exports.default.fetch(`${BASE}/api/session`)).json<{ budgetMode: string }>()).budgetMode;
}

async function firstRunMode(mode?: "rule_based"): Promise<string> {
  const agent = await incidentAgent(env, newId());
  const { runId } = await agent.init({ workspaceId: newId(), target: "https://shop.example.com/", origin: "chat", message: "why?", ...(mode ? { mode } : {}) });
  return (await agent.getRunContext(runId!)).mode;
}

describe("budget guard (TDD §11)", () => {
  afterEach(async () => {
    await env.DB.prepare("DELETE FROM llm_usage WHERE day = ?").bind(today()).run();
  });

  it("stays on AI below 9,000 neurons today", async () => {
    await setToday(BUDGET_GUARD_AT - 1);
    expect(await budgetMode()).toBe("ai");
    expect(await firstRunMode()).toBe("ai");
  });

  it("switches new runs and the session to rule-based at 9,000", async () => {
    await setToday(BUDGET_GUARD_AT);
    expect(await budgetMode()).toBe("rule_based");
    expect(await firstRunMode()).toBe("rule_based");
    expect(await firstRunMode("rule_based")).toBe("rule_based");
  });
});

import { Button, cn, LinkButton, type ButtonProps, type LinkButtonProps } from "@cloudflare/kumo";
import { DashedLoader } from "./DashedLoader";

/**
 * Kumo's `loading` prop draws its own spinner. Spread this instead so a busy button shows our `DashedLoader` in
 * place of its icon and can't be clicked again: `<Button {...loadingProps(busy, Icon, disabled)}>`.
 */
export function loadingProps(loading: boolean | undefined, icon: ButtonProps["icon"], disabled?: boolean) {
  return loading
    ? { loading: false, disabled: true, "aria-busy": true, icon: <DashedLoader size={14} label={null} /> }
    : { loading: false, disabled, icon };
}

/**
 * The app's main action: Kumo's primary (Cloudflare orange) button as a pill (TDD §12.2, §12.4).
 * Same props as Kumo's `Button`; `variant` is fixed; `loading` shows the dashed loader.
 */
export function CtaButton({ className, loading, icon, disabled, ...props }: ButtonProps) {
  return <Button {...props} {...loadingProps(loading, icon, disabled)} variant="primary" className={cn("justify-center rounded-full", className)} />;
}

/** `CtaButton` that navigates (an anchor), for links such as "New incident". */
export function CtaLinkButton({ className, ...props }: LinkButtonProps) {
  return <LinkButton {...props} variant="primary" className={cn("justify-center rounded-full", className)} />;
}

<details><summary>Tool result</summary>

```
---

## 60-second walkthrough

1. Open the app. A workspace is created for you (an anonymous cookie, no sign-up).
2. Type `diagnose cloudflare.com`. Check cards fill in (DNS, then HTTP), and a healthy diagnosis streams in with evidence.
3. In the **Demo service** panel, switch on **500 errors**.
4. Ask "what's wrong with my demo service?". You get an `origin_5xx` diagnosis citing `[http-1]`, plus an approval card: **Restore the demo service to healthy**.
5. Click **Approve**. The demo is restored, a re-check passes, and the incident goes to `mitigated`, then to `resolved` after two more passing checks.

A link to an incident opened by someone else is **view-only**: you can watch it live, but you can't send messages or approve anything.

## How it meets the assignment

| Requirement | Implementation |
|---|---|
| **LLM** | Workers AI `@cf/openai/gpt-oss-120b`, falling back to `@cf/zai-org/glm-4.7-flash`, both routed through **AI Gateway** (`incident-copilot`) for logs and fallback. A deterministic rule engine takes over when both are unavailable. |
| **Workflow / coordination** | **Workflows**: `DiagnosisWorkflow` (extends the Agents SDK's `AgentWorkflow`) runs each LLM turn and each check as its own durable, retryable step, then waits for human approval with `waitForApproval`. `WatchWorkflow` confirms a watched endpoint's failure (2 of 3 checks) before opening an incident on its own. |
| **User input** | A React chat UI (Cloudflare's Kumo design system) served by **Workers Static Assets**, with live updates over a WebSocket straight to the incident's Durable Object. |
| **Memory / state** | **Durable Objects** via the Agents SDK: one `IncidentAgent` per incident with its own SQLite (checks, evidence, messages, approvals, timeline). **D1** holds the workspaces, the incident index per workspace (for the sidebar), the watchlist and the daily neuron count. |

Also used: Cron Triggers (the watchlist), Rate Limiting bindings, Service Bindings with RPC (main Worker → demo Worker), Workers Logs, and the `global_fetch_strictly_public` flag.
```

</details>

import { Hono } from "hono";
import { incidentAgent } from "../agent/stub";
import type { AppEnv } from "../env";
import { isId, newId } from "../lib/ids";
import { ipKey, takeRun } from "../safety/quota";
import { parseTarget } from "../safety/target";
import { demoTarget } from "./session";

type IndexRow = { id: string; target: string; status: string; origin: string; unread: number; updated_at: number };

const MAX_MESSAGE = 2000;

/** Incident API (TDD §7.1). Every route is scoped to the caller's workspace cookie. */
export const incidents = new Hono<AppEnv>()
  .post("/", async (c) => {
    const body = await c.req.json<{ message?: unknown }>().catch(() => ({}) as { message?: unknown });
    const message = typeof body.message === "string" ? body.message.trim().slice(0, MAX_MESSAGE) : "";
    if (!message) return c.json({ error: "empty_message" }, 400);
    const workspaceId = c.get("workspaceId");
    const demoUrl = demoTarget(c.env.DEMO_BASE_URL, workspaceId);
    const target = parseTarget(message, demoUrl ?? "https://demo.invalid/");
    if (target.ok && target.source === "demo" && !demoUrl) return c.json({ error: "demo_unavailable" }, 503);
    if (!target.ok) return c.json({ error: target.error }, 400);
    const quota = await takeRun(c.env, { workspaceId, ip: await ipKey(c.req.header("CF-Connecting-IP")) });
    if (!quota.ok) return c.json({ error: "rate_limited", retryAfter: quota.retryAfter }, 429, { "Retry-After": String(quota.retryAfter) });
    // `?smoke=rule` skips the LLM (TDD §15): the deploy smoke test costs no neurons. It can only make a run cheaper.
    const mode = c.req.query("smoke") === "rule" ? "rule_based" : "ai";
    const id = newId();
    const agent = await incidentAgent(c.env, id);
    await agent.init({ workspaceId, target: target.url, origin: "chat", message, mode });
    return c.json({ id });
  })
  .get("/", async (c) => {
    const { results } = await c.env.DB.prepare(
      "SELECT id, target, status, origin, unread, updated_at FROM incidents_index WHERE workspace_id = ? ORDER BY updated_at DESC LIMIT 50",
    )
      .bind(c.get("workspaceId"))
      .all<IndexRow>();
    return c.json(results.map((r) => ({ id: r.id, target: r.target, status: r.status, origin: r.origin, unread: r.unread === 1, updatedAt: r.updated_at })));
  })
  .get("/:id", async (c) => {
    const id = c.req.param("id");
    if (!(await owns(c.env.DB, c.get("workspaceId"), id))) return c.json({ error: "not_found" }, 404);
    const agent = await incidentAgent(c.env, id);
    return c.json(await agent.getSnapshot());
  })
  .post("/:id/read", async (c) => {
    const id = c.req.param("id");
    if (!(await owns(c.env.DB, c.get("workspaceId"), id))) return c.json({ error: "not_found" }, 404);
    await (await incidentAgent(c.env, id)).markRead();
    return c.body(null, 204);
  });

async function owns(db: D1Database, workspaceId: string, id: string): Promise<boolean> {
  if (!isId(id)) return false;
  return !!(await db.prepare("SELECT 1 FROM incidents_index WHERE id = ? AND workspace_id = ?").bind(id, workspaceId).first());
}

10725-    "@cf/meta/llama-3.2-11b-vision-instruct": Base_Ai_Cf_Meta_Llama_3_2_11B_Vision_Instruct;
/** Hono context type. `Env` is generated by `pnpm cf-typegen` from both wrangler configs. */
export type AppEnv = {
  Bindings: Env;
  Variables: { workspaceId: string };
};
import type { RunMode } from "../agent/state";
import { BUDGET_GUARD_AT, NEURON_DAILY } from "../loop/budget";

/** Daily neuron accounting (TDD §7.4). The budget guard (`budgetMode`) reads `today()`. */
export type UsageSink = {
  add(neurons: number): Promise<void>;
  /** Workers AI said the daily allocation is used up: treat the day as spent. */
  exhausted(): Promise<void>;
  today(): Promise<number>;
};

export const noUsage: UsageSink = { add: async () => {}, exhausted: async () => {}, today: async () => 0 };

const utcDay = (now: number) => new Date(now).toISOString().slice(0, 10);

/** Backed by `llm_usage(day, neurons_est)`. Accounting never breaks a diagnosis, so write errors are only logged. */
export function d1Usage(db: D1Database, now: () => number = Date.now): UsageSink {
  const write = async (sql: string, n: number) => {
    try {
      await db.prepare(sql).bind(utcDay(now()), n).run();
    } catch (e) {
      console.error("llm_usage write failed", (e as Error).message);
    }
  };
  return {
    add: (neurons) =>
      write(
        "INSERT INTO llm_usage (day, neurons_est) VALUES (?1, ?2) ON CONFLICT(day) DO UPDATE SET neurons_est = neurons_est + excluded.neurons_est",
        Math.ceil(neurons),
      ),
    exhausted: () =>
      write(
        "INSERT INTO llm_usage (day, neurons_est) VALUES (?1, ?2) ON CONFLICT(day) DO UPDATE SET neurons_est = max(neurons_est, excluded.neurons_est)",
        NEURON_DAILY,
      ),
    today: async () => {
      const row = await db.prepare("SELECT neurons_est FROM llm_usage WHERE day = ?1").bind(utcDay(now())).first<{ neurons_est: number }>();
      return row?.neurons_est ?? 0;
    },
  };
}

/**
 * Budget guard (TDD §11): from 9,000 estimated neurons today (UTC), new runs skip the LLM. A failed read
 * means "ai": the LLM path still falls back to rules when Workers AI says the allocation is used up.
 */
export async function budgetMode(usage: UsageSink): Promise<RunMode> {
  try {
    return (await usage.today()) >= BUDGET_GUARD_AT ? "rule_based" : "ai";
  } catch (e) {
    console.error("llm_usage read failed; budget guard off", (e as Error).message);
    return "ai";
  }
}

    res = await f("/api/incidents", { method: "POST", headers: { "content-type": "application/json" }, body: JSON.stringify({ message }) });
  } catch {
    return { ok: false, error: "network" };
  }
  if (res.ok) return { ok: true, id: ((await res.json()) as { id: string }).id };
  const body = (await res.json().catch(() => ({}))) as { error?: string; retryAfter?: unknown };
  if (res.status === 429) {
    const seconds = typeof body.retryAfter === "number" ? body.retryAfter : Number(res.headers.get("Retry-After"));
    return { ok: false, error: "rate_limited", retryAfter: Number.isFinite(seconds) && seconds > 0 ? seconds : 60 };
  }
  const known = ["need_target", "empty_message", "demo_unavailable"] as const;
  const error = known.find((k) => k === body.error);
  return { ok: false, error: error ?? "server" };
}

export async function listIncidents(f: Fetch = fetch): Promise<IncidentRow[]> {
  try {
    const res = await f("/api/incidents");
    return res.ok ? ((await res.json()) as IncidentRow[]) : [];
  } catch {
    return [];
  }
}

/** Also creates the workspace cookie on a first visit; use `firstSession` for that first call. */
export async function getSession(f: Fetch = fetch): Promise<Session | null> {
  try {
    const res = await f("/api/session");
    return res.ok ? ((await res.json()) as Session) : null;
  } catch {
    return null;
  }
}

/**
 * The first visit's session call, made once per page load (StrictMode runs effects twice). Each request
 * without a cookie creates its own workspace, so the app waits for this one before making any other call.
 */
export function sessionOnce(f: Fetch = fetch): () => Promise<Session | null> {

### 🤖 Claude · 16:44:40

WP-21, WP-22 and WP-23 are built and pushed to `dev`, one commit each. Nothing is merged to `main` or deployed. All 684 tests pass (up from 604), green three runs in a row, and typecheck and lint are clean. I checked each one in a local browser with the mock AI.

**WP-21: certificate and provider status checks** (`33b8467`)
- **When they run:** as you chose, only when useful. The certificate check runs when HTTPS connections fail. The provider check runs when a site hosted on Cloudflare, GitHub Pages or AWS is failing. The AI can still ask for either one anytime.
- **What I changed from the plan:**
  - **Wildcard certificates:** looking up just the host in crt.sh misses wildcard certificates, so it also looks up `*.parent`. That's 2 requests, where the TDD said 1.
  - **Only major or critical incidents count as an outage:** Cloudflare almost always has a minor incident open, and your demo answers as a Cloudflare server. Without this, every broken demo would have been diagnosed as a Cloudflare outage. The automatic provider check also never runs on your own demo.
- **A limit to know:** certificate logs show certificates that were issued, not the one a site actually serves. `expired.badssl.com` is covered by a valid wildcard in the logs, so the card says "valid" and the diagnosis stays inconclusive. That's the honest result.
- **Local check:** the cards show up, and a repeat lookup came from the cache in 0.0 s. The "crt.sh down → unavailable" case is covered by tests with a fake crt.sh, not by a real outage.

**WP-22: postmortem** (`944a87c`)
- **How it's written:** as you chose, the impact window, detection and timeline come from the incident's records, and the AI writes the summary, root cause, resolution and follow-ups. Any section the AI leaves out comes from a template.
- **Cost:** a saved postmortem is free to reopen. A new one counts against the run limit.
- **Header button:** "Postmortem" is greyed out until the incident is resolved. Someone with a view-only link can read a saved postmortem but can't write one.
- **Local check:** I broke the demo, approved the fix, and it resolved 30 s later. The postmortem then showed, the copied text matched, and it was still there after a reload.
- **Bug found and fixed while checking:** a site that was healthy at its first check was described as "recovered after 3 checks".

**WP-23: voice** (`3289689`)
- **How it works:** click to start, click to stop, and it stops by itself at 30 s. The text goes into the input box and is never sent on its own.
- **Limits:** the voice limit applies per workspace and per visitor IP, like the run limit. Voice turns off when the daily AI budget runs low.
- **Local check:** with a fake microphone, "is demo dot example dot com down" became "is demo.example.com down" in the box, and nothing was sent.
- **Not tested:** a real call to the speech-to-text model, because it uses some of the daily AI budget. I can run one with a short clip if you say yes.

**After your next `dev` → `main` merge:**
- Check that the crt.sh cache works on the deployed workers.dev site. The docs were unclear about this.
- Run the postmortem step on the live site.

The README "Designed, not built" table now lists only WP-24 to WP-26. The board and the TDD are updated.

### 🤖 Claude · 17:09:49

The deploy for your merge passed (checks, deploy and the smoke test), so the new code is live. Both checks are done in the live app in your browser.

**1. Does the crt.sh cache work on workers.dev?**

The certificate check only runs on its own when HTTPS fails to connect, so use a host with a broken certificate:

1. Start a new incident: `is expired.badssl.com down?` and wait for the Certificate card.
2. Note the incident ID from the address bar (`/i/<id>`). In the same browser, open `https://<your-app>/api/incidents/<id>`. Your cookie makes it work in that browser only.
3. Find the check with `"tool": "cert_info"`. Its `finishedAt` minus `startedAt` is how long the lookup took, in milliseconds. Expect a few seconds, because crt.sh is slow.
4. Within the hour, start a second incident for the same host and look again.
   - If the second time is close to 0 ms, the cache works on workers.dev.
   - If it takes seconds again, the cache does nothing there. That's harmless, and I'd note it in the TDD.

While you're there, read the HTTP probe card. If it says **"TLS error on 3/3 samples"**, live Workers report certificate errors clearly, which the local runtime doesn't. That's still an open question in the TDD, so tell me either way.

**2. Postmortem (walkthrough step 6)**

1. In the Demo service panel, pick **500 errors**.
2. Ask `what's wrong with my demo service?`, then click **Approve** on the card.
3. Wait for **Resolved**, about 30 s.
4. Click **Postmortem**. The text should stream in, then show the full markdown. Check that:
   - the summary is written by the AI, not "Mock postmortem…";
   - the root cause cites an ID like `[http-1]`;
   - the timeline times (UTC) match what you just did.
5. Click **Copy markdown** and paste it somewhere. Then reload the page, click **Postmortem** again, and it should open the same text without writing it again.

**Cost:** about 130 neurons per diagnosis plus about 50 for the postmortem, so roughly 450 for everything above. Each incident and the new postmortem also counts toward the run limit of 3 a minute.

import type { IncidentStatus } from "./state";

/** Post-action monitor (D11, TDD §8.4): quick checks on agent schedule ticks, never inside a Workflow. */
export const MONITOR_TICK_S = 15;
export const MONITOR_MAX_TICKS = 10;
/** Passes in a row that resolve a mitigated incident. The verify check after an action counts as the first. */
export const MONITOR_PASSES = 3;

export type MonitorState = { ticksLeft: number; passes: number };
export type TickResult = {
  monitor: MonitorState | null;
  /** The status to move to, if it changes. */
  to: IncidentStatus | null;
};

/**
 * One tick, pure. A pass counts up (and recovers an investigating incident to mitigated); 3 in a row resolve
 * it. A fail resets the count and reopens a mitigated incident. The monitor ends when resolved or out of ticks.
 */
export function tick(state: MonitorState, status: IncidentStatus, pass: boolean): TickResult {
  const ticksLeft = state.ticksLeft - 1;
  const passes = pass ? state.passes + 1 : 0;
  let to: IncidentStatus | null = null;
  if (pass && status === "investigating") to = "mitigated";
  else if (pass && status === "mitigated" && passes >= MONITOR_PASSES) to = "resolved";
  else if (!pass && status === "mitigated") to = "investigating";
  const done = to === "resolved" || ticksLeft <= 0 || status === "resolved";
  return { monitor: done ? null : { ticksLeft, passes }, to };
}

Based on the search results, here's what I found about the `cloudflare_ai_gateway` resource in Terraform Cloudflare Provider v5:

## Overview

The `cloudflare_ai_gateway` resource was introduced to manage AI Gateway instances. It was added in Terraform Provider v5.19.0 (released April 24, 2026).

## Import Support

The search results indicate that cf-terraforming is a CLI utility that automates the conversion of existing Cloudflare resources into Terraform configurations by retrieving resource data via the Cloudflare API to generate HCL code or import commands. The tool supports resources released in version 5 of the Cloudflare Terraform Provider, which would include the `cloudflare_ai_gateway` resource.

For importing Cloudflare resources into your Terraform state, you can use cf-terraforming generate to generate the Terraform configuration for your resources.

However, the search results don't contain specific documentation about the exact import syntax or resource structure for the `cloudflare_ai_gateway` resource. For detailed import instructions and resource configuration details, I recommend checking the official Cloudflare Terraform provider documentation or the provider's GitHub repository.

## WP-24: `recheck_burst` (re-check every minute for 15 minutes)

### Monitor (`worker/agent/monitor.ts`, `IncidentAgent.ts`)
- Add per-kind settings: `MONITOR_KINDS = { post_action: { tickS: 15, maxTicks: 10 }, burst: { tickS: 60, maxTicks: 15 } }` (`BURST_TICK_S`/`BURST_MAX_TICKS` per TDD §5.2). The pure `tick()` reducer is unchanged (pass while investigating → mitigated, 3 in a row → resolved, fail → back to investigating).
- `startMonitor(kind, passes)` stores `kind`; `monitorTick` reschedules with that kind's `tickS` (today it always uses `MONITOR_TICK_S`). `monitor_started`/`monitor_tick` events carry `kind`. Watch checks keep deferring to a running monitor; `resolve()` already stops it; a later restore's post-action monitor replaces a burst (`startMonitor` stops the old one).

### Action (`worker/actions/catalog.ts`)
- `ProposeContext.monitorRunning` (new). `proposable("recheck_burst")`: status investigating/awaiting_approval and no monitor running.
- `pickAction` rule actions: `inconclusive` → `recheck_burst`, then `add_to_watchlist`; other non-healthy → `add_to_watchlist` (unchanged). Restore still wins on the demo.
- Run by the agent (it owns the scheduler), like `generate_postmortem`: `executeAction` starts the burst monitor (passes 0) with outcome text "Re-checking every minute for 15 minutes. 3 passing checks in a row resolve it."; `afterAction` takes the existing "done, nothing to re-check" path → back to investigating.

### UI (`web/components/IncidentView.tsx`)
- The monitor line reads by kind: post-action keeps "Watching after the fix: …"; burst says "Re-checking every minute: n of 3 passed in a row · k checks left".

### Tests
- Unit: `monitor`/`catalog` (burst proposable rules, inconclusive picks burst, a running monitor blocks it, restore still wins).
- Integration (`watch-agent`/`approval` style): a real-site inconclusive incident → burst card → approve → monitor `kind: burst`, next tick scheduled ~60 s out (`getSchedules`), driving `monitorTick` by RPC: pass → mitigated, 3 passes → resolved, ticks in the timeline with `kind: burst`.
- **Done when (local, mock AI, real network):** a 404 page (e.g. `cloudflare.com/does-not-exist`) is inconclusive → card → Approve → the snapshot timeline shows `monitor_tick` events about 60 s apart (watched for ~3 min).

- `main.tf`: `cloudflare_d1_database` (`incident-copilot`) and `cloudflare_ai_gateway` (`incident-copilot`), each with an `import` block for the **existing** resource (D1 ID `d24601d5-…` from `wrangler.jsonc`), so apply adopts them and creates nothing. Attribute names and import ID formats taken from the provider's registry docs at build time; gateway settings set to what the dashboard created (logs on, no cache, no rate limit) so the plan shows no changes.

  /** One monitor tick (D11, TDD §8.4), run by the agent scheduler. Also callable over RPC, which the tests use. */
  async monitorTick(): Promise<void> {
    const m = this.sql<MonitorRow>`SELECT * FROM monitor WHERE id = 1`[0];
    if (!m) return;
    const incident = this.mustIncident();
    const v = await this.quickCheck();
    const r = tick({ ticksLeft: m.ticks_left, passes: m.consecutive_passes }, incident.status, v.pass);
    this.event("monitor_tick", { pass: v.pass, passes: v.pass ? m.consecutive_passes + 1 : 0, ticksLeft: m.ticks_left - 1 });
    if (r.monitor) {
      const next = await this.schedule(MONITOR_TICK_S, "monitorTick");
      this.sql`UPDATE monitor SET ticks_left = ${r.monitor.ticksLeft}, consecutive_passes = ${r.monitor.passes}, schedule_id = ${next.id} WHERE id = 1`;
    } else {
      this.sql`DELETE FROM monitor WHERE id = 1`;
      if (r.to !== "resolved") this.event("monitor_ended", { reason: "out of ticks" });
    }
    if (r.to) {
      // Automatic changes are new activity for the sidebar's unread dot (FR-50); the user's own aren't.
      this.sql`UPDATE incident SET unread = 1 WHERE id = ${this.name}`;
      await this.transition(r.to, r.to === "resolved" ? "3 passing checks in a row" : v.pass ? "recovered" : "failing again");
    }
    else {
      this.touch();
      this.syncState();
    }
  }


  private quickCheck(): Promise<QuickCheckResult> {
    const allowOrigin = devAllowOrigin(this.env);
    return quickCheck(this.mustIncident().target, { fetch: (input, init) => fetch(input, init), ...(allowOrigin ? { allowOrigin } : {}) });
  }

  private async startMonitor(passes: number) {
    await this.stopMonitor();
    const s = await this.schedule(MONITOR_TICK_S, "monitorTick");
    this.sql`INSERT INTO monitor (id, kind, ticks_left, consecutive_passes, schedule_id) VALUES (1, 'post_action', ${MONITOR_MAX_TICKS}, ${passes}, ${s.id})`;
    this.event("monitor_started", { ticks: MONITOR_MAX_TICKS, everySec: MONITOR_TICK_S });
    this.syncState();
  }

  private async stopMonitor() {
    const m = this.sql<MonitorRow>`SELECT * FROM monitor WHERE id = 1`[0];
    if (!m) return;
    if (m.schedule_id) await this.cancelSchedule(m.schedule_id);
    this.sql`DELETE FROM monitor WHERE id = 1`;
  }

  private incident(): IncidentRow | undefined {
    try {
      return this.sql<IncidentRow>`SELECT * FROM incident LIMIT 1`[0];

# Cloudflare D1 Database Resource

## Example Usage

```terraform
resource "cloudflare_d1_database" "example_d1_database" {
  account_id = "023e105f4ecef8ad9ca31a8372d0c353"
  name = "my-database"
  jurisdiction = "eu"
  primary_location_hint = "wnam"
  read_replication = {
    mode = "auto"
  }
}
```

## Arguments

**Required:**
- `account_id` (String) - Account identifier tag
- `name` (String) - D1 database name

**Optional:**
- `fields` (List of String) - Comma-separated list of fields for response filtering
- `jurisdiction` (String) - Location restriction for data storage. Values: "eu", "fedramp", "us"
- `primary_location_hint` (String) - Region for primary creation. Values: "wnam", "enam", "weur", "eeur", "apac", "oc"
- `read_replication` (Attributes) - D1 read replication configuration:
  - `mode` (String, Required) - Replication mode. Values: "auto", "disabled"

## Attributes (Read-Only)

- `created_at` (String) - ISO8601 creation timestamp
- `file_size` (Number) - Database size in bytes
- `id` (String) - D1 database identifier (UUID)
- `uuid` (String) - D1 database identifier (UUID)
- `version` (String) - Version identifier
- `num_tables` (Number, Deprecated) - Table count (no longer accurate)

## Import Syntax

```shell
terraform import cloudflare_d1_database.example '<account_id>/<database_id>'
```
````

## Arguments Summary

### Required Arguments
- `account_id` (String) - The account ID
- `id` (String) - "Unique identifier of the AI Gateway within the account"
- `cache_invalidate_on_update` (Boolean)
- `cache_ttl` (Number)
- `collect_logs` (Boolean)
- `rate_limiting_interval` (Number)
- `rate_limiting_limit` (Number)

### Optional Arguments
- `authentication` (Boolean)
- `byok_only` (Boolean) - "Requires customer-provided provider credentials and prevents fallback to Unified Billing"
- `dlp` (Attributes) - Data loss prevention settings
- `guardrails` (Attributes) - Prompt and response guardrails
- `log_classification` (Boolean)
- `log_management` (Number)
- `log_management_strategy` (String) - Values: "STOP_INSERTING", "DELETE_OLDEST"
- `logpush` (Boolean)
- `logpush_public_key` (String)
- `otel` (Attributes List) - OpenTelemetry configuration
- `rate_limiting_technique` (String) - Values: "fixed", "sliding"
- `retry_backoff` (String) - Values: "constant", "linear", "exponential"
- `retry_delay` (Number) - Milliseconds (0-60000)
- `retry_max_attempts` (Number) - Range: 1-5
- `spend_limits` (Attributes) - Spending limit rules
- `store_id` (String)
- `stripe` (Attributes) - Stripe integration settings
- `workers_ai_billing_mode` (String) - Values: "postpaid", "unified"
- `zdr` (Boolean)

### Read-Only Arguments
- `created_at` (String)
- `is_default` (Boolean)
- `modified_at` (String)

## Import Syntax

```shell
terraform import cloudflare_ai_gateway.example '<account_id>/<id>'
```

```json
{
  "command": "mkdir -p infra && cat > infra/versions.tf <<'EOF'\nterraform {\n  # Import blocks with expressions need 1.6.\n  required_version = \">= 1.6\"\n\n  required_providers {\n    cloudflare = {\n      source = \"cloudflare/cloudflare\"\n      # 5.19 is the first version with cloudflare_ai_gateway.\n      version = \"~> 5.19\"\n    }\n  }\n}\n\n# Reads CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN from the environment: the token never goes in a file.\nprovider \"cloudflare\" {}\nEOF\ncat > infra/variables.tf <<'EOF'\nvariable \"account_id\" {\n  description = \"Cloudflare account ID (wrangler whoami). Set it in terraform.tfvars, which is gitignored.\"\n  type        = string\n}\n\nvariable \"d1_name\" {\n  description = \"D1 database name, as in wrangler.jsonc.\"\n  type        = string\n  default     = \"incident-copilot\"\n}\n\nvariable \"d1_database_id\" {\n  description = \"ID of the existing D1 database (wrangler.jsonc d1_databases[0].database_id), adopted by the import below.\"\n  type        = string\n  default     = \"d24601d5-e047-48c8-8d5a-66880d841b40\"\n}\n\nvariable \"gateway_id\" {\n  description = \"AI Gateway ID, as in wrangler.jsonc vars.AI_GATEWAY_ID.\"\n  type        = string\n  default     = \"incident-copilot\"\n}\nEOF\ncat > infra/main.tf <<'EOF'\n# Terraform owns the two account-level resources the Workers depend on; wrangler owns the Workers, their\n# bindings, Durable Objects, Workflows, cron and migrations (TDD §15, D15). Both resources already exist\n# (created by wrangler and the dashboard on Day 1), so they're imported, not created. State is local.\n\nimport {\n  to = cloudflare_d1_database.main\n  id = \"${var.account_id}/${var.d1_database_id}\"\n}\n\nresource \"cloudflare_d1_database\" \"main\" {\n  account_id = var.account_id\n  name       = var.d1_name\n\n  # Holds every workspace, incident index, watch and webhook: never let a plan delete it.\n  lifecycle {\n    prevent_destroy = true\n  }\n}\n\nimport {\n  to = cloudflare_ai_gateway.main\n  id = \"${var.account_id}/${var.gateway_id}\"\n}\n\n# Every LLM call goes through this gateway (env.AI.run(..., { gateway })). Settings match how it was created in\n# the dashboard: logs on, no cache, no rate limit (the app enforces its own limits, TDD §11).\nresource \"cloudflare_ai_gateway\" \"main\" {\n  account_id                 = var.account_id\n  id                         = var.gateway_id\n  collect_logs               = true\n  cache_ttl                  = 0\n  cache_invalidate_on_update = false\n  rate_limiting_interval     = 0\n  rate_limiting_limit        = 0\n\n  lifecycle {\n    prevent_destroy = true\n  }\n}\nEOF\ncat > infra/outputs.tf <<'EOF'\noutput \"database_id\" {\n  description = \"Goes in wrangler.jsonc d1_databases[0].database_id.\"\n  value       = cloudflare_d1_database.main.id\n}\n\noutput \"gateway_id\" {\n  description = \"Goes in wrangler.jsonc vars.AI_GATEWAY_ID.\"\n  value       = cloudflare_ai_gateway.main.id\n}\nEOF\ncat > infra/terraform.tfvars.example <<'EOF'\n# Copy to terraform.tfvars (gitignored) and fill in. The API token comes from CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN instead.\naccount_id = \"<your Cloudflare account ID, from `npx wrangler whoami`>\"\nEOF\npython3 - <<'EOF'\np='.gitignore'; s=open(p).read()\ns=s.replace(\"infra/.terraform.lock.hcl\\n\",\"infra/.terraform.lock.hcl\\ninfra/terraform.tfvars\\n\")\nopen(p,'w').write(s)\nEOF\ngrep -n infra .gitignore; V=$(curl -s https://checkpoint-api.hashicorp.com/v1/check/terraform | python3 -c 'import sys,json;print(json.load(sys.stdin)[\"current_version\"])'); echo \"terraform $V\"; SP=/private/tmp/claude-502/-Users-sahil-personal-cloudflare-infra-chatbot/9cf3ba95-c415-4e8d-98f9-43bfb7e50880/scratchpad; mkdir -p $SP/tf && cd $SP/tf && curl -s -o tf.zip \"https://releases.hashicorp.com/terraform/${V}/terraform_${V}_darwin_arm64.zip\" && unzip -oq tf.zip && ./terraform version | head -1",
  "description": "Write infra files and fetch a local Terraform binary",
  "timeout": 120000
}

146-## CI/CD
147-
148:- **[`ci.yml`](.github/workflows/ci.yml)** runs on every PR into `main`: install with a frozen lockfile, typecheck, lint, test. The Terraform `fmt`/`validate` steps switch on once `infra/` exists.
149-- **[`deploy.yml`](.github/workflows/deploy.yml)** runs on every push to `main`. It re-runs CI, applies the D1 migrations, builds, and deploys the **demo Worker first** (the main Worker's Service Binding needs it to exist), then the main Worker. Last, it runs `scripts/smoke.ts` against the new URL: health, session, demo set to healthy, a rule-based diagnosis, and a check that it comes back `healthy`. The smoke test uses no neurons.
150-- **Secrets** (GitHub Actions): `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN` (the "Edit Cloudflare Workers" template plus Account › D1 › Edit) and `CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID`. Nothing else is needed.
151-- **Code review:** work happens on `dev`, and every `dev` → `main` PR is also reviewed by Sift (`sift-review.yml`).
152-
153-**First deploy by hand** (TDD §15):
154-1. `wrangler d1 create incident-copilot`, then put its ID into `wrangler.jsonc`.
155-2. Create the AI Gateway `incident-copilot` in the dashboard.
156-3. `wrangler d1 migrations apply DB --remote`.
157-4. `pnpm build`, then `wrangler deploy -c dist/incident_copilot_demo/wrangler.json`.
158-5. Set `DEMO_BASE_URL` in `wrangler.jsonc` to the demo's URL.
159-6. `pnpm build`, then `wrangler deploy`.
160-7. For Slack/Discord webhooks, set the encryption key once: `openssl rand -base64 32 | npx wrangler secret put WEBHOOK_KEY`. Without it, webhooks stay off.
161-
162:## Terraform and wrangler: who owns what
163-
164-| Owner | Resources | Why |
165-|---|---|---|
166:| **Terraform** (planned, local state, D15) | Long-lived account resources: the D1 database, and the AI Gateway if the provider supports it | Created once and rarely changed |
167-| **wrangler** (`wrangler.jsonc`) | Worker code, every binding, Durable Object migrations, Workflows, Static Assets, D1 schema migrations | Changes with every deploy, and some bindings (Rate Limiting) exist only in wrangler |
168-
169:Today, Terraform isn't built (WP-25, below the cut line). D1 was created with `wrangler d1 create`, and the AI Gateway in the dashboard (only a gateway named `default` is created automatically). Whether the Terraform provider can manage AI Gateway is still an open question (PRD R1). CI's Terraform steps switch on by themselves once `infra/*.tf` exists.
170-
171-## Designed, not built (yet)
172-
173-The full design for each of these is in the TDD. Day 2 of the build is still in progress, so items leave this list as they ship ([`TASKS.md`](TASKS.md) has live status).
174-
175-| WP | Feature | Design |
176-|---|---|---|
177:| 25 | Terraform for D1 (and the AI Gateway if supported) | TDD §15 |
178-| 26 | Demo GIF of the walkthrough | — |
179-
180-## Repository layout
181-
182-```
183-worker/       main Worker: api/ (Hono routes), agent/ (IncidentAgent, state, transitions, monitor),
184-              workflows/ (DiagnosisWorkflow), loop/ (planning loop, budget, findings, narration),
185-              tools/, safety/ (SSRF, reader, origin pin, target parser), llm/, diagnosis/ (rules), actions/
186-web/          React client: components/, hooks/, lib/
187-demo-service/ the breakable demo Worker (modes, DemoState DO, DemoAdmin RPC)
188-migrations/   D1 schema
189-scripts/      smoke.ts
190-eval/         LLM eval: cases.ts (cases + scoring), run.ts, results.md
191-test/         unit/, integration/, demo/, fixtures/ (recorded LLM responses)
````

</details>

Expect **2 to import, 0 to add, 0 to change, 0 to destroy**. ### 🤖 Claude · 17:40:45

WP-24 and WP-25 are done and pushed to `dev`. WP-26 (the GIF) needs one more merge from you, because the first recording on the live site turned up a bug.

**WP-24: re-check every minute** (`0d1be49`)
- **When the card shows:** an inconclusive result on a real site (often a flaky one) now gets "Re-check every minute for 15 minutes" ahead of the watchlist, as you chose. The AI can also suggest it for any failure, and the demo still gets Restore.
- **What approving does:** checks the site every minute for 15 minutes; 3 passes in a row resolve the incident. The header reads "Re-checking every minute: n of 3…".
- **Local check:** on a real 404 page, the ticks came 60.3, 60.0 and 60.1 s apart. 687 tests passed at that point.

**WP-25: Terraform** (`d14953b`)
- `infra/` adopts your existing D1 database and AI Gateway through `import` blocks, so nothing new is created. Both are protected from deletion.
- `fmt` and `validate` pass locally. CI will run them on your next PR.
- **Your part:** the apply steps are in the README. Expect `terraform plan` to show **2 to import, 0 to add, 0 to change, 0 to destroy**. If it wants to change or replace anything, stop and paste me the plan.

**WP-26: the bug the first take found** (`a25ba58`)
- **What went wrong:** on the healthy `cloudflare.com` run, the AI checked the AWS status page itself. AWS has had two Middle East regional events open since March, and we counted every AWS event as an outage, so a healthy site showed a red **Fail** card. The extra AI turn also made the run take 82 s.
- **Fix:** AWS events older than 24 hours are listed but no longer count as an outage, and the AI is told to check status pages only when the site is failing. 688 tests pass, three runs in a row.
- I didn't use that take. It cost roughly 300 neurons.

**What I need from you:** merge `dev` → `main` so the fix goes live. Then tell me and I'll make one recording, which costs about 300 neurons. ### 🤖 Claude · 17:46:56

**1. What Terraform does, and whether it can recreate or delete anything**

The files in `infra/` describe the two Cloudflare resources the app depends on: the D1 database and the AI Gateway. Wrangler keeps owning everything else (the Workers, bindings and deploys), and Terraform never touches those.

Both resources already exist, so each one has an `import` block. That tells Terraform to adopt the existing resource instead of creating a new one. Adopting just reads it from Cloudflare and saves a record of it in a local file on your machine.

It can't delete or replace either one:
- **`prevent_destroy`** is set on both. If a plan would ever delete or replace them (for example, a setting that can only be chosen at creation doesn't match), Terraform stops with an error instead.
- **`terraform plan` changes nothing.** It only shows what would happen.
- **`terraform apply` asks you to type `yes`** before doing anything.

The only real change it could make is an in-place settings update on the gateway, if my settings (logs on, no cache, no rate limit) don't match yours. The plan would show that line by line, and you could say no.

**2. Is it fine if you don't run it?**

Yes, for the app. Nothing reads Terraform's output, deploys keep using wrangler, and the live site is unaffected.

It isn't purely a test, though. It's the "infrastructure as code" part of the project, and WP-25's done-when includes "applied once". CI's check only confirms the files are valid; it never talks to your account.

So you have three choices:
- **Skip it:** I mark the apply as "not run" on the board and in the README. The files still count as the design.
- **Run `terraform plan` only:** read-only, it proves the files match your account (it should say "2 to import, 0 to add, 0 to change, 0 to destroy"). It needs the API token from the README steps.
- **Run plan, then apply:** after a clean plan, apply only saves the adopted resources in your local state file and changes nothing on Cloudflare.

Tell me which one you pick, and whether you've merged so I can record the GIF.

### 🤖 Claude · 17:48:29

Run these in your own terminal, so the token never enters this chat.

**1. Install Terraform (one time)**
```sh
brew tap hashicorp/tap
brew install hashicorp/tap/terraform
terraform version        # needs 1.6 or newer
```

**2. Create an API token**

In the Cloudflare dashboard, go to **My Profile → API Tokens → Create Token → Create Custom Token**:
- **Permissions:** `Account · D1 · Edit` and `Account · AI Gateway · Edit`
- **Account Resources:** Include → your account
- Create it and copy the token. Cloudflare shows it only once.

**3. Set up the folder**
```sh
cd ~/personal/cloudflare-infra-chatbot/infra
export CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN='<SECRET>'
cp terraform.tfvars.example terraform.tfvars
npx wrangler whoami      # shows your Account ID
```
Open `terraform.tfvars` and replace the placeholder so it reads `account_id = "<your account ID>"`. The file is gitignored, so it won't be committed.

**4. Plan (read-only, changes nothing)**
```sh
terraform init
terraform plan
```
The last line should be:
```
Plan: 2 to import, 0 to add, 0 to change, 0 to destroy.
```
- **If it says exactly that,** go to step 5.
- **If it shows any change, replace, or a `prevent_destroy` error,** stop and paste me the output. The output doesn't contain the token.

**5. Apply**
```sh
terraform apply          # shows the same plan; type: yes
terraform output
```
`terraform output` should print `database_id = "d24601d5-e047-48c8-8d5a-66880d841b40"` and `gateway_id = "incident-copilot"`, the same values as in `wrangler.jsonc`.

**6. Clean up**
```sh
unset CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN
```
- **Keep `infra/terraform.tfstate`.** It's Terraform's record that it now manages these two resources. It's gitignored and stays on your machine.
- **Optional:** delete the token in the dashboard if you won't run Terraform again.

When you're done, tell me the result and I'll mark WP-25 as applied.

### 🤖 Claude · 17:50:48

Yes, with one change and one condition.

**The change:** that token (the one in the GitHub secret `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN`) was made from the "Edit Cloudflare Workers" template plus `Account · D1 · Edit`. As far as I know that template doesn't include AI Gateway, so the plan would fail with a permission error when it reads the gateway. Add it:
- **Dashboard:** My Profile → API Tokens → that token → **Edit** → add `Account · AI Gateway · Edit` → **Continue to summary → Update token**.
- **The value stays the same,** so the GitHub secret keeps working.

**The condition:** you still need the token's value. GitHub never shows a saved secret again, so if you didn't keep a copy, you can't reuse it. Either create a new token as in step 2, or use **Roll** on the old token to get a new value. Rolling invalidates the old value, so you'd then also have to update the GitHub secret, or deploys will fail. A fresh token is simpler.

One trade-off: adding AI Gateway access to the deploy token also gives your GitHub deploys that permission. That's low risk. A separate token keeps the two apart, and you can delete it afterwards.

Everything else in the steps stays the same: `export CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN='…'`, then `terraform init` and `terraform plan`.

# Incident Copilot

An AI on-call assistant that runs entirely on Cloudflare. You describe a symptom in chat ("checkout.example.com is throwing errors"). An agent then picks which checks to run, runs them as durable Workflow steps and explains in plain English what's wrong, with every claim citing its evidence (`[http-1]`). It may propose a fix, and the fix runs only after you approve it.

It comes with a **demo service** you can break on purpose (500 errors, slowness, a redirect loop, bad caching), so the whole loop of break → diagnose → approve → verified → resolved can be tried in about a minute.

> **Live URL:** not published yet. The rate limits and the neuron budget guard (WP-17) are built and go live with the next deploy; the URL is shared after that (decision D2, see [Security](#security)).

- **Docs:** [`PRD.md`](PRD.md) (what and why), [`TDD.md`](TDD.md) (how, and every decision), [`TASKS.md`](TASKS.md) (the build board), [`PROMPTS.md`](PROMPTS.md) (how AI was used).

# Session transcripts

Full transcripts of the Claude Code sessions that built Incident Copilot, exported per session (PRD §14). [`PROMPTS.md`](../PROMPTS.md) is the running log of every prompt; these files hold the whole conversations behind it.

## Day 1 (2026-10-15)

Times are local (IST). The first session is the planning before Day 1 (PRD and TDD).

| Transcript | Local time | User messages | Session title |
|---|---|---|---|
| [2026-10-14-1647-6128303f.md](2026-10-14-1647-6128303f.md) | 2026-10-14 16:47 → 2026-10-15 00:12 | 9 | PRD implementation planning |
| [2026-10-15-0009-45c103a1.md](2026-10-15-0009-45c103a1.md) | 2026-10-15 00:09 → 00:16 | 2 | Task board from TDD.md |
| [2026-10-15-0156-a5d17ca9.md](2026-10-15-0156-a5d17ca9.md) | 2026-10-15 01:56 → 02:07 | 8 | Workflow files for CI and code review |
| [2026-10-15-0019-26bdd860.md](2026-10-15-0019-26bdd860.md) | 2026-10-15 00:19 → 02:08 | 15 | PROMPT_HISTORY.md file |
| [2026-10-15-1335-0085bc8e.md](2026-10-15-1335-0085bc8e.md) | 2026-10-15 13:35 → 13:39 | 2 | cf_ai_incident_copilot pull request 2 review |
| [2026-10-15-1342-3eb514ab.md](2026-10-15-1342-3eb514ab.md) | 2026-10-15 13:42 → 16:47 | 8 | WP-06 continuation |
| [2026-10-15-1648-5ac328ad.md](2026-10-15-1648-5ac328ad.md) | 2026-10-15 16:48 → 19:48 | 9 | Incident copilot handoff |

The last session was still running when it was exported (Day 1 wrap-up). Its remaining turns go into the Day 2 export.

## What each file contains

- Every user message, every reply from Claude, every tool call with its full input, and every tool result in full, in order and with timestamps.
- System reminders that the editor added to user messages are kept, folded under "system reminder".

## What was left out or changed

- **Secrets were removed and replaced with placeholders:**
  - `<ACCOUNT_ID>` for the Cloudflare account ID, including partial copies;
  - `<CLIENT_IP>` for the developer's IP address, which appeared in `wrangler tail` request logs;
  - `<TOKEN>`, `<API_KEY>`, `<GITHUB_TOKEN>` and `<SECRET>` for any token or key. None were found, but the export checks for them.
- **Not exported, because these aren't conversation text:**
  - screenshots and attached documents, which are binary;
  - the model's private reasoning blocks;
  - the editor's own bookkeeping entries (titles, file snapshots, queue events).

Nothing else was edited.

### 🤖 Claude · 00:11:12

I've created the task board at [TASKS.md](TASKS.md). It covers all 26 work packages from the design doc's 2-day plan, and the design doc itself is unchanged.

**What's in it:**
- **Now:** a short block showing the current task, the next one, hours used today against plan, and any blockers. It's rewritten on every update.
- **One table per day:** each task has a status (⬜ todo, 🔄 doing, ✅ done, ⛔ blocked, ✂️ cut), start and finish times, and the commit hash. Day 2 has the cut line between WP-20 and WP-21, plus the rule that WP-12 and WP-13 move to the start of Day 2 if Day 1 runs over.
- **Checklists:** a collapsible block per task with sub-steps taken from the design doc's scope, ending with its "done when" check.
- **Decisions log and daily wrap-ups:** for spike findings, slips, cuts, and confirming the transcript was exported to `prompts/` each day.

I've also saved a memory so future sessions keep the board updated: mark a task 🔄 when it starts, ✅ with the commit hash when it's done, and log anything that changes.

**The design doc's time totals don't match its own estimates.** Adding up each task's estimate:
- **Day 1:** 14.75 h, not "about 14 h".
- **Day 2:** 11.5 h, not "about 13 h" (8.25 h before the cut line, 3.25 h after).

The board uses the real sums. So Day 1 is a bit tighter than stated, and Day 2 has about 1.5 h of slack.

The first task is WP-01 (Scaffold).

datetime.datetime.fromisoformat(ts.replace(\"Z\", \"+00:00\")).astimezone(IST)\n\nindex = []\nfor path in sorted(glob.glob(f\"{SRC}/*.jsonl\"), key=os.path.getmtime):\n    sid = os.path.basename(path)[:-6]\n    entries = [json.loads(l) for l in open(path) if l.strip()]\n    titles = [e.get(\"aiTitle\") or e.get(\"title\") for e in entries if e.get(\"type\") == \"ai-title\"]\n    title = next((t for t in reversed(titles) if t), \"session\")\n    stamps = [e[\"timestamp\"] for e in entries if e.get(\"timestamp\")]\n    start, end = local(stamps[0]), local(stamps[-1])\n    md = [f\"# Session {sid[:8]}: {title}\", \"\",\n          f\"- Session ID: `{sid}`\", f\"- Local time (IST): {start:%Y-%m-%d %H:%M} → {end:%Y-%m-%d %H:%M}\",\n          \"- Exported from Claude Code. Full transcript; only secrets are removed (see `prompts/README.md`).\", \"\"]\n    turns = 0\n    for e in entries:\n        t, m = e.get(\"type\"), e.get(\"message\")\n        if t not in (\"user\", \"assistant\") or not isinstance(m, dict): continue\n        when = local(e[\"timestamp\"]).strftime(\"%H:%M:%S\") if e.get(\"timestamp\") else \"\"\n        content = m.get(\"content\")\n        if isinstance(content, str): content = [{\"type\": \"text\", \"text\": content}]\n        for b in content or []:\n            bt = b.get(\"type\")\n            if t == \"user\" and bt == \"text\":\n                if e.get(\"isMeta\"): continue\n                turns += 1\n                md += [f\"## 👤 User · {when}\", \"\", fold_reminders(b.get(\"text\", \"\")), \"\"]\n            elif t == \"user\" and bt == \"tool_result\":\n                md += [f\"<details><summary>Tool result{' (error)' if b.get('is_error') else ''}</summary>\", \"\", fence(result_text(b.get(\"content\"))), \"\", \"</details>\", \"\"]\n            elif t == \"user\" and bt in (\"image\", \"document\"):\n                md += [f\"_[{bt} attached, omitted]_\", \"\"]\n            elif t == \"assistant\" and bt == \"text\":\n                md += [f\"### 🤖 Claude · {when}\", \"\", b.get(\"text\", \"\"), \"\"]\n            elif t == \"assistant\" and bt == \"tool_use\":\n                md += [f\"**Tool call: `{b.get('name')}`**\", \"\", fence(json.dumps(b.get(\"input\"), indent=2, ensure_ascii=False), \"json\"), \"\"]\n            # thinking blocks are the model's private reasoning and are not exported\n    name = f\"{start:%Y-%m-%d-%H%M}-{sid[:8]}.md\"\n    open(os.path.join(OUT, name), \"w\").write(redact(\"\\n\".join(md)))\n    index.append((name, start, end, title, turns))\n\nwith open(os.path.join(OUT, \"index.json\"), \"w\") as f:\n    json.dump([{\"file\": n, \"start\": f\"{s:%Y-%m-%d %H:%M}\", \"end\": f\"{e:%Y-%m-%d %H:%M}\", \"title\": t, \"userMessages\": u} for n, s, e, t, u in index], f, indent=2)\nprint(json.dumps(counts))\nfor n, s, e, t, u in index: print(f\"{n:42} {u:3} user msgs  {t}\")\nEOF\nmkdir -p $SP/prompts-export && python3 $SP/export.py $SP/prompts-export && du -sh $SP/prompts-export/* | sort -k2",
